\displaystyle 0=\sigma_{1}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1})+\sigma_{2}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})+\sigma_{3}\pi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1})

\displaystyle\text{ for all }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}\in I_{1},\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}\in\tilde{I}_{1},\,\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}+\sigma_{3}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}.

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle 0=\mu_{1}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1})+\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}\pi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1})

\displaystyle\text{ for all }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}\in I_{1},\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}\in\tilde{I}_{1},\,\mu_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}=\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}-\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}.

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle 0=\mu_{1}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1})-c_{1}\pi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1})

\displaystyle\text{ for all }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}\in I_{1},\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}\in\tilde{I}_{1},\,\mu_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}+c_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}.

The second equation follows since
\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})=0
as that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}\in\verts(\mathcal{P}_{q})
and we also multiple through by
\sigma_{1}\mu_{1}
. The third equation follows from rewriting based on definitions. We will now show the equivalence between the two 7-tuples. Suppose
\pi
is additive over
(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}})
. That is,

\displaystyle 0=\mu_{1}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1})+\mu_{2}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2})+\mu_{3}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3})

\displaystyle\text{ for all }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}\in I_{1},\,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2}\in I_{2},\,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}\in I_{3},\,\mu_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}+\mu_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2}+\mu_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}.

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle 0=\tilde{\mu}_{1}\pi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1})+\mu_{2}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2})+\mu_{3}\pi({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3})

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}\in\tilde{I}_{1},\,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2}\in I_{2},\,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}\in I_{3},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}+\tilde{\mu}_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{x}$}}^{1}+\mu_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2}+\mu_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}},

where the equivalence follows from applying the last equation above. This is equivalent to
\pi
is additive over
(\tilde{I}_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}})
.

Validity.

Finally, we show that
(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}})

is a valid 7-tuple if and only if

(\tilde{I}_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}))

is a valid 7-tuple.
We establish the correspondence via
2.3
. First note that since

(I_{1},{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}\},\tilde{I}_{1},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
is a valid 7-tuple, by
2.3
, we have

I_{1}=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}-\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}\tilde{I}_{1}
.

Suppose
2.3
(
1
) holds for
(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}})
.
Multiplying by
\mu_{1}
, we have

\mu_{1}I_{1}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}-(\mu_{2}I_{2}+\mu_{3}I_{3})
.
Then substituting for
I_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}-\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}\tilde{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}-(\mu_{2}I_{2}+\mu_{3}I_{3}),

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle-\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}\tilde{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}-\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\mu_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}-(\mu_{2}I_{2}+\mu_{3}I_{3}),

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{1}\tilde{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}-(\mu_{2}I_{2}+\mu_{3}I_{3}).

Therefore,
2.3
(
1
) holds for
(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}})
if and only if condition 1 of
2.3
(
1
) holds for
(\tilde{I}_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{\mu}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\tilde{u}$}})
. Conditions 2 and 3 are similar. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.1
.

Suppose that
\bm{\tau}=(I,{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})\in\mathcal{E}
where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\in\tfrac{1}{q}\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
Suppose that
\bm{\tau}^{\prime}=(I,J^{\prime},K^{\prime},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}
containing
I
. Then there exists a valid 7-tuple
\bm{\tau}^{\prime\prime}=(K,J^{\prime},K^{\prime},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}^{\prime\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{\prime\prime})
with
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}^{\prime\prime}=(\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{3})
, where
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{1}=-\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}\sigma^{\prime}_{1}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{\prime\prime}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{\prime}-\sigma^{\prime}_{1}\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+\sigma^{\prime}_{1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}
, such that
\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}\setminus\{\bm{\tau}^{\prime}\}\cup\{\bm{\tau}^{\prime\prime}\}
is an equal update.

Proof.

It follows directly from
7.1
.
∎

Lemma 7.1

(Step 4 equivalence)
.

Suppose
\bm{\tau}=({{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
is the only 7-tuple in
\mathcal{E}
where
I
appears and where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\in\frac{1}{q}\mathcal{Z}^{2}
. Then there exist 7-tuples
\{\bm{\tau}^{1},\bm{\tau}^{2},\bm{\tau}^{3}\}
of type (2) such that the update
\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{E}\setminus\{\bm{\tau}\}\cup\{\bm{\tau}^{1},\bm{\tau}^{2},\bm{\tau}^{3}\}
,
\mathcal{Z}\leftarrow\mathcal{Z}
,
\mathcal{P}\leftarrow\mathcal{P}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{P}\setminus\{I\}
is an equivalent update. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}=\{\bm{\tau}^{1}=

\displaystyle({{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},{{\text{}}}K_{1},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}),

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}^{2}=

\displaystyle({{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},{{\text{}}}K_{2},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}),

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}^{3}=

\displaystyle({{\text{}}}I_{3},{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},{{\text{}}}K_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})\},

where
I_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}},I_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}},I_{3}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}
are the edges of
I
and
K_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}},K_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}},K_{3}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}
are the edges of
K
.

Proof.

Since

\bm{\tau}=({{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
, by
2.3
is a vaild 7-tuple, we have

I=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}+\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}K).

Since this equation is just an affine transformation, we must also have

I_{i}=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}+\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}K_{i}).

Then by
2.3
, the three 7-tuples in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}:=\{\bm{\tau}^{1},\bm{\tau}^{2},\bm{\tau}^{3}\}
are valid 7-tuples.

Define the projection
h\colon\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}\to\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus I}
such that
\theta\mapsto\theta|_{\bigcup(\mathcal{P}\setminus I)}
.

Suppose
\theta\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
and
\theta\neq 0
.
Clearly
h(\theta)
is continuous since
h
is just the restriction to a closed set.
By
6.0
, since
\theta
is additive on
\bm{\tau}
, then it is also additive on all of the 7-tuples in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
. Therefore,
\theta\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}\subseteq\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
. This inclusion holds since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
. Since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
has no 7-tuples containing
I
, the restriction
\theta|_{\bigcup\mathcal{P}\setminus I}
must also be additive on all 7-tuples in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
Thus
h(\theta)\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus I}
.
Also
h(\theta)\neq 0
because otherwise
\theta=0
on
\mathcal{P}\setminus I
and then by
\bm{\tau}\in\mathcal{E}
, we have
\theta=0
on
I
as well.

Now define the lift
h^{-1}\colon\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus I}\to\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
by

\hat{\theta}\mapsto\theta

where

\theta({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})=\begin{cases}\hat{\theta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})&\text{ for }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\bigcup(\mathcal{P}\setminus I)=\bigcup\mathcal{P}\setminus\intr(I),\\
-\sigma_{3}\sigma_{1}\hat{\theta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})&\text{ for }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\intr(I),\text{with }\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}+\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}.\end{cases}

Above, whenever
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\intr(I)
, we have
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\intr(K)
.
Hence, the values of
\theta
on
\intr(I)
are determined uniquely by the additive relation
({{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

Now consider any
\hat{\theta}\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus I}
,
\hat{\theta}\neq 0
and let
\theta=h^{-1}(\hat{\theta})
. By construction of
h^{-1}
, we see that
\theta\neq 0
and
\theta
is additive on
\bm{\tau}
in the interior. Since
\hat{\theta}
, and hence
\theta
, is additive on the 7-tuples in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
,
\theta
is also additive on the closed set, that is

\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sigma_{i}\theta({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{i})=0,\ \forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}\in I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}\in K,\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sigma_{i}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}.

Since
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\in\verts(\mathcal{P}_{q})
is only one point, this equation shows a affine relationship between the values
\theta
on
K
and on
I
. Since
\theta
is continuous on the closed set
K
, then it is also continuous on the closed set
I
. Thus,
\theta=h^{-1}(\hat{\theta})\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
.

Therefore, the update is equivalent.
∎

Corollary 7.1

(Updates applied iteratively)
.

By iteratively applying
7.1
, we create an equivalent update with
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}},{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

For a tuple
\bm{\tau}=({{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
, we will apply
7.1
to all the tuples in
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}},{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
containing
I
. Thus, after some equivalent updates,
\bm{\tau}
will be the only tuple containing
I
, and the projection
p(\mathcal{E})
will not change. If
I\in\mathcal{Z}
, we will apply
7.1
to replace
I
with
K
in
\mathcal{Z}
.
Then, we can apply
7.1
to remove
I
from the domain
\mathcal{P}
. By iteratively applying these procedures, we will remove all the tuples in
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}},{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
.
∎

Step 5: Remove
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
by exchanging 7-tuples, inferring zeros, and changing the domain.

\Leftrightarrow

Step 5a. Canonical representation

Consider
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
.
Suppose
\bm{\tau}=({{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}J,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.
We now rewrite this by using edges of
K
. For this, we need extra 7-tuples with edges and a point to relate values on corresponding edges. These follow from using continuity arguments, and we can use the notation to absorb the point into the
\textstyle\bf t
argument and assume the point is
\textstyle\bf 0
.

[Use of continuity]

\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}\setminus\{\bm{\tau}\}\cup\{\bm{\tau}^{1},\bm{\tau}^{2},\bm{\tau}^{3}\}

\Rightarrow

Step 5b(i).
Two canonical tuples with two edges of the same triangle. We can use hidden interval lemma
4.0
to deduce an affine property. This allows us to set the edge in common to 0. This zero together with an additivity implies the other additivity.

Note that if all three canonical tuples exist, we can apply this step twice. This actually induces the function to be zero on the entire triangle.

\bm{\tau}^{1},\bm{\tau}^{2}
with common
{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K
triangle and common edge
{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}I
.

\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}\setminus\{\bm{\tau}^{2}\}

\mathcal{Z}\leftarrow\mathcal{Z}\cup\{{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}I\}

\Leftrightarrow

Step 5b(ii).
Exactly 1 tuple of this type
In this case, we can just project out the triangle and record the formula update as a combination of function values from the edges.

\mathcal{P}\leftarrow\mathcal{P}\setminus\{{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K\}

Now we assume
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}=\emptyset
.

\Rightarrow
Thus,
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}}\cup\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}}
(Type (2) and (3))

There are three types of separability relations. After permutation, the 7-tuples that are separability relations look like

(1)
({{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}J,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
,

(2)
({{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}J,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
,

(3)
({{\text{}}}I,{{\text{}}}J,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

We next show that every separability relation including two edges
I,J
and a triangle
K
can be exchanged for separability relation including two edges of
K
and the triangle
K
, along with relations between the edges of
K
and the edges
I,J
.

Lemma 7.1

(Step 5a equal)
.

For each
\bm{\tau}=({\text{}}I,{\text{}}J,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})\in\mathcal{E}
with
K\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
, and
I\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{\text{}}}
,
J\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{\text{}}}
for distinct
{\text{}},{\text{}}\in\{{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}\}
,
there exists a set of 7-tuples

\begin{array}[]{r@{\hskip-.5pt}l@{\hskip-.4pt}rrrl}\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}=\{\bm{\tau}^{1}=\hskip-0.5pt&(\hfil\hskip-0.4pt&{\text{}}K_{1},&{\text{}}K_{2},&{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,&\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}}),\\
\bm{\tau}^{2}=\hskip-0.5pt&(\hfil\hskip-0.4pt&{\text{}}I,&\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}\},&{\text{}}K_{1},&\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}),\\
\bm{\tau}^{3}=\hskip-0.5pt&(\hfil\hskip-0.4pt&\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},&{\text{}}J,&{\text{}}K_{2},&\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})\}\end{array}

where
K_{1},K_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
are the edges of
K
such that
K_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{\text{}}}
for
i=1,2
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\in\verts(I)
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}\in\verts(J)
are chosen by
3.0
such that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}

is a set of valid 7-tuples,
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}}=(1,1,-1)
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}}=\sigma_{3}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}-\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}})
.
Then the update
\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime}:=(\mathcal{E}\setminus\{\bm{\tau}\})\cup\{\bm{\tau}^{1},\bm{\tau}^{2},\bm{\tau}^{3}\}
is equal.

Proof.

Consider any
\bar{\pi}\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
. Since
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(I,J,K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
,
by
6.0
, we also have that
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(I,\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}\},K_{1},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
and
(\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},J,K_{2},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

Therefore, since
(I,\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}\},K_{1},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}),(\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},J,K_{2},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, finishing the proof is equivalent to showing the following claim:

Claim:
Let
\bar{\pi}\colon\bigcup\mathcal{P}\to\mathbb{R}
with
\bar{\pi}_{\mathcal{P}_{q}}\equiv 0
such that
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(I,\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}\},K_{1},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
and
(\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},J,K_{2},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

Then
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(I,J,K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
if and only if
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(K_{1},K_{2},K,(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}})
.

Claim proof:

Since
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(I,\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}\},K_{1},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
and
(\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\},J,K_{2},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
, we have the following two equations:

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})+\sigma_{2}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime})+\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in I,\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in K_{1},\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}+\sigma_{3}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}})+\sigma_{2}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in I,\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in K_{2},\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}+\sigma_{3}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}.

Since
\bar{\pi}|_{\frac{1}{q}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\equiv 0
, these become the following

(19)

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})

\displaystyle=-\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})

\displaystyle\forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in I,\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in K_{1},\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}+\sigma_{3}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}},

(20)

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})

\displaystyle=-\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})

\displaystyle\forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in I,\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in K_{2},\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}+\sigma_{3}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}.

Now suppose
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(I,J,K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
. This is equivalent to

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})+\sigma_{2}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})=0

\displaystyle\forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in I,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in J,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in K,\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}+\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}},

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle-\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})-\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\sigma_{3}\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})=0

\displaystyle\forall\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in K_{1},\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in K_{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in K,

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}-\sigma_{3}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})+({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}-\sigma_{3}\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}},

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})+\bar{\pi}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})-\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})=0

\displaystyle\forall\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in K_{1},\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in K_{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in K,\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}},

The first equivalence, we use (
19
) to substitute for
\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
and (
20
) to substitute for
\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
. The second is just a rewriting
where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}}=\sigma_{3}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{\prime}-\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}})
.
By definition, this means
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
(K_{1},K_{2},K,(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}})
.
Therefore, the claim is proved and we are done.
∎

We now prove a more complicated lemma on just having two separability relations.

Lemma 7.1

(Step 5b(i) equal)
.

Suppose that we have a set of valid 7-tuples

\{\bm{\tau}^{1}=({\text{}}K_{1},{\text{}}K_{2},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}),\bm{\tau}^{2}=({\text{}}K_{1},{\text{}}K_{3},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{2})\}\subseteq\mathcal{E}

with
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}}=(1,1,-1)
,
K\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
, and
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
be the edges of
K
such that
K_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{\text{}}}
for
i=1,2,3
.
Then the update
\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime}:=(\mathcal{E}\setminus\{\bm{\tau}^{2}\})
,
\mathcal{Z}\leftarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{Z}\cup\{{\text{}}K_{1}\}
is equal.

Proof.

We will prove this for the case where
K_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}
,
K_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}
,
K_{3}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}
. The other cases are similar so we will omit their proofs.

First, suppose
\bar{\pi}\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
. By
4.0
,
\bar{\pi}
is affine on
K_{1}
and is
-affine on
K
. Since
\bar{\pi}
is zero on
\verts(\mathcal{P}_{q})
, this implies that
\bar{\pi}
is zero on all of
K_{1}
. Thus,
\bar{\pi}\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}}
.

Next, suppose
\bar{\pi}\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}}
. We just need to show that
\bar{\pi}
is additive on
\bm{\tau}^{2}
.

By
3.0
, we can assume that

\begin{array}[]{r@{\hskip-.5pt}rrlr@{\hskip-.25pt}l@{\hskip-.25pt}l@{\hskip-.5pt}rrr@{\hskip-.5pt}r}F(\hskip-0.5pt&{{\text{}}}K_{1},&{{\text{}}}K_{2},&{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,&\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1})\hskip-0.25pt&=\hfil\hskip-0.25pt&\{({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1})+\frac{1}{q}(\hfil\hskip-0.5pt&\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1},&\lambda_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{2},&(\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{2}))\hskip-0.5pt&:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}\},\\
F(\hskip-0.5pt&{{\text{}}}K_{1},&{{\text{}}}K_{3},&{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,&\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{2})\hskip-0.25pt&=\hfil\hskip-0.25pt&\{({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{2})+\frac{1}{q}(\hfil\hskip-0.5pt&-\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1},&\lambda_{2}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{2}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1}),&(-\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1}+\lambda_{2}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{2}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1})))\hskip-0.5pt&:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}\},\end{array}

where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}=\sigma{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}}^{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}=\sigma{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}}^{2},\sigma\in\{\pm 1\}
. Then
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{2}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\frac{1}{q}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}
,
K_{3}=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\frac{1}{q}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}):\lambda\in[0,1]\}
.

Since
\bar{\pi}
is additive over
({{\text{}}}K_{1},{{\text{}}}K_{2},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\mu$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\mu$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}),
we have

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1})+

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2})-\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2})=0

\displaystyle\forall\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2})-\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2})=0

\displaystyle\forall\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}(1-\lambda_{2}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2})-\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2})=0

\displaystyle\forall\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}){\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1})+

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}))-\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}+\tfrac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2})=0

\displaystyle\forall\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}

The second equation follows since
\bar{\pi}
is zero on
K_{1}
. Therefore, the value of
\bar{\pi}
on
K
is the constant along horizontal lines. Thus, we can obtain the third equation.
Because
\bar{\pi}
is zero on
K_{1}
, we obtain the last equation, which is exactly the definition of
\bar{\pi}
being additive on
\bm{\tau}^{2}
.
∎

Lemma 7.1

(Step 5b(ii) equivalence)
.

Let
K\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
be a triangle with edges
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
. Therefore,
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}\subseteq K
.
Suppose
\bm{\tau}^{0}=(K_{1},K_{2},K,(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
is the
only
7-tuple in
\mathcal{E}
containing
K
. Then for
\bm{\tau}^{1}=(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3},(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
, the update

\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}^{\prime}:=\left(\mathcal{E}\setminus\{\bm{\tau}^{0}\}\right)\cup\{\bm{\tau}^{1}\},\ \ \mathcal{P}\leftarrow\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\}

is equivalent.

Proof.

We first show that
\bm{\tau}^{0}
is a valid 7-tuple given that
\bm{\tau}^{1}
is a valid 7-tuple.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}\in K_{1}\cap K_{2}
and consider the sets
\bar{K}_{1}=K_{1}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}
,
\bar{K}_{2}=K_{2}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}
,
\bar{K}_{3}=K_{3}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}
, and
\bar{K}=K-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}
.
Since
(K_{1},K_{2},K,(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
is a valid 7-tuple, by
2.3
we have

(\bar{K}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})\subseteq-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+(\bar{K}_{2}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})+(\bar{K}_{1}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}})

thus

\bar{K}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+\bar{K}_{2}+\bar{K}_{1}
.
By the geometry of
\mathcal{P}_{q}
, since
\bar{K},\bar{K}_{1},\bar{K}_{2}
all touch the origin and
\bar{K}_{1},\bar{K}_{2}
are edges of
K
, this relation can only hold if
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf v$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf v$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}
. Therefore

\bar{K}\subseteq\bar{K}_{2}+\bar{K}_{1}.

Clearly
\bar{K}_{3}\subseteq\bar{K}\subseteq\bar{K}_{2}+\bar{K}_{1}
, which is condition 3 of
2.3
.
Furthermore, it can be checked that every triangle
\hat{K}
in
\mathcal{P}_{q}
touching the origin with edges
\hat{K}_{1},\hat{K}_{2},\hat{K}_{3}
where
\hat{K}_{1},\hat{K}_{2}
both touch the origin, that
