\hat{K}_{1}\subseteq\hat{K}_{3}+(-\hat{K}_{2})
and
\hat{K}_{2}\subseteq\hat{K}_{3}+(-\hat{K}_{1})
.
Applying this to
\bar{K}_{1},\bar{K}_{2},\bar{K}_{3}
and substituting back to
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}
then shows conditions 1 and 2 of
2.3
. Therefore,
(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3},(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
is a valid 7-tuple.

Define the projection
h\colon\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}\to\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\}}
such that
\theta\mapsto\theta|_{\bigcup(\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\})}
.

Suppose
\theta\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
.
Clearly
h(\theta)
is continuous since
h
is just the restriction to a closed set.
By
6.0
, since
\theta
is additive over
(K_{1},K_{2},K,(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
, then it is also additive over
\bm{\tau}^{1}=(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3},(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
. Therefore,
\theta\in\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}\cup\{\bm{\tau}^{1}\}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}\subseteq\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
. This inclusion holds since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}\cup\{\bm{\tau}^{1}\}
. Since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
has no 7-tuples containing
K
, the restriction
\theta|_{\bigcup(\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\})}
must also be additive on all 7-tuples in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
Thus
h(\theta)\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\}}
.

Now define the lift
h^{-1}\colon\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\}}\to\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
by

\hat{\theta}\mapsto\theta

where
\theta({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})=\hat{\theta}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
for all
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\bigcup(\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\})=\bigcup\mathcal{P}\setminus\intr(K)
, and
\theta
is uniquely defined on
\intr(K)

by the equation

\displaystyle\theta({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})+\theta({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})-\theta({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\forall\ {\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in K_{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in K_{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in\intr(K),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}.

Now consider any
\hat{\theta}\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}\setminus\{K\}}
and let
\theta=h^{-1}(\hat{\theta})
. By construction of
h^{-1}
, we see that
\theta
is additive on the (non-valid) 7-tuple

(K_{1},K_{2},\intr(K),(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
. Since
\hat{\theta}
, and hence
\theta
, is additive on the 7-tuples in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
.
Specifically,
\theta
is additive on

(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3},(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
. Also, since
\theta|_{\mathcal{P}_{q}}=0
, it follows trivially that
\theta
is additive on the (non-valid) 7-tuples
(K_{1},K_{2},K_{1},(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
and
(K_{1},K_{2},K_{2},(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.
Therefore, we obtain that
\theta
is additive on the valid 7-tuple
(K_{1},K_{2},K,(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.
Since
\theta
is continuous on
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
it follows by construction that
\theta
is continuous on
K
.
Therefore,
\theta\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
.

∎

After all the previous steps, we can assume that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}}\cup\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of valid 7-tuples,
\mathcal{P}
a collection of faces contains all faces in
\mathcal{P}_{q}
, and
\mathcal{Z}=\verts(\mathcal{P}_{q})
. Then there exists

•

a collection of faces
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
that is a union of faces in
\mathcal{P}_{q}
and contains all faces in
\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}
and possibly some faces of
\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
, and

•

a set
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
of
\pi
-additive 7-tuples containing no 7-tuples with triangles (Furthermore, we can assume
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
only contain
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{{{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
)

•

a set
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\supseteq\mathcal{Z}
(possibly some faces of
\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
),

such that
(
18
) holds.

\Leftrightarrow

Step 6a. Case: Empty triangle

Suppose there exists a triangle
{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I\in\mathcal{P}
with
I\notin p(\mathcal{E})
and
i\notin\mathcal{Z}
.

Then there exists a perturbation on this triangle.

Then this perturbation implies a lifting to a perturbation function in the original space.

[Contradiction by perturbation]

Derives a contradiction!

\Leftrightarrow

Step 6b. Case: No empty triangles
Suppose now that we can project out all triangles. Then we have a system that is actually a 1-dimensional system. Thus we can apply
5.0
to show that the remaining infinite system of equations is nontrivial if and only if a finite system of equations is nontrivial. This implies a perturbation on the finite system that maps to a subset of
\tfrac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.

Derives a contradiction!

This completes the proof!

From the previous steps, we can assume that
\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\neq\{0\}
, and we only need to show that
{\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}}\neq\{0\}
. If there is a nontrivial
\bar{\pi}_{mq}\in{\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}}
, by
6.0
, we also have a continuous piecewise linear function
\bar{\pi}
in
\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
constructed from the interpolation of
\bar{\pi}_{mq}
, whose restriction on
\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}
is
\bar{\pi}_{mq}
.
And we can lift
\bar{\pi}
back to
\bar{\pi}^{0}\in\widehat{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{P}}
such that the restriction of
\bar{\pi}^{0}|_{\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\in\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}_{\mathcal{Z}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2},\mathcal{P}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}
is nonzero because it is a lift of
\bar{\pi}_{mq}
.

Step 6a. Interior Triangle Perturbation:

Suppose there exists a triangle
{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}K\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
and
K\notin\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
.
Let
\bar{\pi}\colon\bigcup\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
such that
\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})=0
for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}})\cap(\bigcup\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2})
, and
\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})=\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}\in\bigcup\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
Then for any such
\bar{\pi}
,
\bar{\pi}
is trivially additive on all valid 7-tuples
(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
where
I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}}}
. Therefore,

\bar{\pi}\in\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2},\bigcup\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}.

In particular, assume that
K=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x_{0}\\
y_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)+\frac{1}{q}\conv(\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\})
. Then because
m\geq 3
, we can define
\bar{\pi}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})=1
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x_{0}\\
y_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)+\frac{1}{mq}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\notin\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
.
Therefore, there exists a non-trivial
\bar{\pi}
for
m\geq 3
in
{\bar{\Pi}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}}
. Thus,
\pi
is not extreme.

Step 6b. Edge perturbation:

We will now assume that
p(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
does not contain any triangles
K\in\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
that is not in
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
(i.e,
K
is not used in any 7-tuple from
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
).

Let
{{\text{}}}K_{1},{{\text{}}}K_{2},{{\text{}}}K_{3}
be the edges of
K
. We update
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
by adding

K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}
and remove
K
. Then by setting
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\setminus\{K\}
we achieve an equivalent update where the map simply sets function values on
K
to zero.

Therefore, we can assume
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}
and only need to show there exists a nontrivial function
\bar{\pi}
on
\mathcal{P}_{q,{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}}\cap\frac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. The function value in the interior of triangles is determined uniquely by interpolation.

The only remaining relations contain edges and points and are of the form of Items
2
,
3
from
3.0
.
We now map the problem to a different space using
5.0
and apply
5.0
. This shows that when looking for nontrivial solutions, it suffices to look at the discrete grid
\tfrac{1}{mq}\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. In particular, we can now look for solutions to a finite problem on the restricted grid. Solutions here exist if and only if solutions on the continuous domain exist.

Finally then, we can conclude that 3
\Rightarrow
1.
∎

As a corollary to this result, we could provide an algorithmic result, akin to
[
10
, Theorem 1.8]
, that shows that in time polynomial in
q
, we can detect if a given piecewise linear function is extreme or a facet. But we don’t feel the need to state that explicitly here.

Appendix A
Postponed Proofs

Proof of
3.0
.

Proof for
F({{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}\},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

We show that the set of additivities is a 1-dimensional space.
Suppose that

I_{3}=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}},\lambda\in\Delta_{1}\},

where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}\in\{(1,0),(0,1),(1,-1)\}
, and
\lambda\in[0,1]
are scalar parameters.
By
2.3
, we know that

I_{1}=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}\}+\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}I_{3}).

Therefore,
I_{1}=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}-\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}\frac{1}{q}\lambda{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}},\lambda\in\Delta_{1}\}
, where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}-\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}
.

If
F(\bm{\tau})
can be parametrized, then
I_{1}=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}\}+\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}I_{3})
. By
2.3
,
\bm{\tau}
is valid.

Furthermore,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}+\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}
is unique.
∎

Proof for
F({{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

We express each point
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{i}\in I_{i}
as:

{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{i}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{i}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{i}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{i},

where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}=(1,0)
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}=(0,1)
, and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{3}=(1,-1)
. Here,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{i}
is a fixed point on the edge
I_{i}
, and
\lambda_{i}\in[0,1]
represents the scalar parameter for each direction
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{i}
.
Since
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3})\in F(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
, we have:

\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sigma_{i}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{i}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3}.

Substituting
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{i}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{i}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{i}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{i}
for each
i
, we obtain:

\sigma_{1}\left({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}\right)+\sigma_{2}\left({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}\right)={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{3}\left({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{3}\right).

Expanding and rearranging, we get:

\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}+\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{q}\left(\sigma_{1}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}+\sigma_{2}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}\right)={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}-\frac{1}{q}\sigma_{3}\lambda_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{3}.

We rearrange to express
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
as a linear system:

\frac{1}{q}\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}&\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{bmatrix}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}-\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}-\frac{1}{q}\sigma_{3}\lambda_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{3}.

Substitute
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1}=(1,0)
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}=(0,1)
, and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{3}=(1,-1)
, and simplify:

\frac{1}{q}\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{1}&0\\
0&\sigma_{2}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{bmatrix}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}-\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}-\frac{1}{q}\sigma_{3}\lambda_{3}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
-1\end{bmatrix}.

Since the matrix

\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{1}&0\\
0&\sigma_{2}\end{bmatrix}
is non-singular with
\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{1}&0\\
0&\sigma_{2}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{1}&0\\
0&\sigma_{2}\end{bmatrix}
,
we can solve for
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
uniquely in terms of
\lambda_{3}
:

\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{bmatrix}=q\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{1}&0\\
0&\sigma_{2}\end{bmatrix}\left({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}-\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}-\frac{1}{q}\sigma_{3}\lambda_{3}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
-1\end{bmatrix}\right)={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{\prime}+\lambda_{3}\begin{bmatrix}-\sigma_{3}\sigma_{1}\\
\sigma_{3}\sigma_{2}\end{bmatrix},

for some
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
Note that the expression is only feasible (as a valid 7-tuple) if for each
i=1,2
we have either
\lambda_{i}=1-\lambda_{3}
or
\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{3}

since we need
\lambda_{i}\in[0,1]
.

Thus
\lambda_{3}

serves as a single parameter from which

\lambda_{1}

and

\lambda_{2}

can be uniquely determined.
Set
\lambda:=\lambda_{3}
.
∎

Proof for

F({{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

This follows similar to
F({{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}{{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.
∎

Proof for

F({{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

Let
I_{i}^{\prime}=\sigma_{i}I_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
. Then
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3})\in F(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
is equivalent to
(\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{1},\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{2},-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{3})\in F(I_{1}^{\prime},I_{2}^{\prime},I_{3}^{\prime},(1,1,-1),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})
.

We express each point
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{i}\in I_{i}^{\prime}
as
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}^{i}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{i}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{i}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}}^{i}
for
i=1,2
, where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{i}
is a vertex of
I_{i}
and
\lambda_{i}\in\Delta_{1}
.
By
2.3
, we know that
I_{3}^{\prime}\subseteq-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+I_{2}^{\prime}+I_{1}^{\prime}
. Thus,

I_{3}^{\prime}
is contained in the parallelogram
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}}^{1}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}}^{2}
, where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}=-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}
,
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in[0,1]
.

If
I_{3}^{\prime}
is a lower triangle
{}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 0$}}}{\text{}}
, then
I_{3}^{\prime}=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}+\frac{1}{q}\left(\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{2}\right),\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}\}
. Therefore,

F({{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})=\{(\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1},\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2},-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3})+\frac{1}{q}(\lambda_{1}\sigma_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{1},\lambda_{2}\sigma_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{2},-\sigma_{3}(\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{2})):\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}\}.

If
I_{3}^{\prime}
is an upper triangle
{}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 0$}}}{\text{}}
, then
I_{3}^{\prime}=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}+\frac{1}{q}\left(\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{2}\right),\mathbf{1}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}\}
, and

F({{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})=\{(\sigma_{1}\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{1},\sigma_{2}\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{2},-\sigma_{3}\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{3})+\frac{1}{q}(-\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\sigma_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{1},-\tilde{\lambda}_{2}\sigma_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{2},\sigma_{3}(\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}^{2})):\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}}\in\Delta_{2}\},

where
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}=1-\lambda_{i}
,
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{i}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{i}+\frac{1}{q}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}}^{i}
,
i=1,2
, and
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{3}=-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}+\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{2}+\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{1}
. Then
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}}^{i}
,
i=1,2,3
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{i}=-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf e$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf e$}}}^{i}
,
i=1,2
will be the desired parameterization.

On the other hand, if
F(\bm{\tau})
can be parametrized

F({{\text{}}}I_{1},{{\text{}}}I_{2},{{\text{}}\,{\text{}}}I_{3},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\sigma$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\sigma$}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}})=\{(\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1},\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2},-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3})+\frac{1}{q}(\lambda_{1}\sigma_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1},\lambda_{2}\sigma_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{2},-\sigma_{3}(\lambda_{1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}^{2})):\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf\lambda$}}\in\Delta_{2}\},

then
\sigma_{1}I_{1}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{3}(I_{3})_{1}
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}+\sigma_{2}I_{2}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-\sigma_{3}(I_{3})_{2}
, where
(I_{3})_{i}=\{-\sigma_{3}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}+\frac{1}{q}\lambda_{i}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{i}):\lambda_{i}\in\Delta_{1}\}
,

.
Thus,
I_{1}=\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}+\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}(I_{3})_{1})\subseteq\sigma_{1}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}I_{2}+\sigma_{1}\sigma_{3}I_{3})
. Similarly,
I_{2}\subseteq\sigma_{2}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1}I_{1}+\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}I_{3})
. Also
I_{3}\subseteq\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}-(\sigma_{3}\sigma_{2}I_{2}+\sigma_{3}\sigma_{1}I_{1})
. By
2.3
,
\bm{\tau}
is valid.

Furthermore, note that we can find
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf d$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf d$}}}^{2}
from

because the three vertices of

is
-\sigma_{3}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}
,

,

. Then we can determine
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}
from

and

.
Thus
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf t$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf t$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{1}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{2}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}^{3}
is unique.

∎

Proof.

Proof of
5.0

We will prove each property of the lemma in turn.

(1) Symmetry of Orbits:

For any
x\in[0,1]
, the orbit
\mathcal{O}(x)
is defined as:

\mathcal{O}(x)=\{(x,i),(1-x,i):i=1,\dots,\ell\}.

By definition, this set includes both
x
and
1-x
for each index
i
. It follows immediately that:

\mathcal{O}(x)=\mathcal{O}(1-x),

because swapping
x
and
1-x
in the orbit definition yields the same set. Thus, the system behaves symmetrically with respect to
x
and
1-x
.

(2) Disjointness of Orbits:

Suppose
x\neq x^{\prime}
and
x,x^{\prime}\in[0,1/2]
. We want to show that:

\mathcal{O}(x)\cap\mathcal{O}(x^{\prime})=\emptyset.

If
x
(or
x^{\prime}
) is
1/2
, then the orbit is unique as
\{(1/2,i):i\in[\ell]\}

By definition, for
x\in[0,1/2)
, the orbit
\mathcal{O}(x)
consists of the pairs
(x,i)
and
(1-x,i)
, where
1-x\in(1/2,1]
. Similarly, the orbit
\mathcal{O}(x^{\prime})
consists of the pairs
(x^{\prime},i)
and
(1-x^{\prime},i)
, where
1-x^{\prime}\in(1/2,1]
.

Since
x\neq x^{\prime}
and both
x
and
1-x
are distinct from
x^{\prime}
and
1-x^{\prime}
, it follows that:

\mathcal{O}(x)\cap\mathcal{O}(x^{\prime})=\emptyset.

This is because the pairs
(x,i)
and
(1-x,i)
are distinct from
(x^{\prime},i)
and
(1-x^{\prime},i)
, as
x\neq x^{\prime}
and
1-x\neq 1-x^{\prime}
. Therefore, the orbits for distinct points in
[0,1/2]
are disjoint.

(3) Union of Orbits for
x\in[0,1/2]
Covers All Points:

We need to show that:

\{(x,i):x\in[0,1],i=1,\dots,\ell\}=\bigsqcup_{x\in[0,1/2]}\mathcal{O}(x).

For any
x\in[0,1]
, we have two cases:
(1) If
x\in[0,1/2]
, then
(x,i)
is part of
\mathcal{O}(x)
.
(2) If
x\in[1/2,1]
, then
(x,i)=(1-x^{\prime},i)
for some
x^{\prime}\in[0,1/2]
, and thus
(x,i)\in\mathcal{O}(x^{\prime})
.

Therefore, every point
(x,i)
in
[0,1]
can be expressed as part of the orbit
\mathcal{O}(x^{\prime})
for some
x^{\prime}\in[0,1/2]
. This shows that the union of orbits for
x\in[0,1/2]
covers the entire set of points
(x,i)
for
x\in[0,1]
.
∎
